Police Radio Resources

A quick listening guide to agency call signs, radio language, officer names, and radio IDs in AFPD recordings.

Call signs Radio language Names and radio IDs Sources and downloads

Agency call signs

The agency part of a spoken unit identifier tells you which department is calling. J is spoken as “Juliet.” For example, the 5J in 5J171 identifies American Fork; it does not identify the officer by itself.

1J
Utah County
2J
Provo
3J
Orem
5J
American Fork
7J
Pleasant Grove
8J
Lehi
13J
Lindon
18J
Lone Peak

Utah County radio referenceUtah State Agency Radio Call Identifiers

Radio language

Short meanings for terms you may hear. Codes and abbreviations can vary by agency; use the surrounding conversation to interpret them.

10-4
OK; message received or acknowledged. Used to acknowledge a transmission.
Code 4
Situation under control; no further assistance needed. It describes the need for assistance, not necessarily the end of the call.
10-9
Repeat the last message. “10-9, my radio cut out” is a request to hear the message again.
10-14
Information. In Utah radio usage, “10-21 for 10-14” means call by phone for information.
10-20 / What’s your 20?
Location. “What’s your 20?” means “Where are you?” “My 20 is…” means “I’m at…” followed by a location.
10-21
Call by phone. A request to continue the conversation by telephone.
10-8
In service; available for calls. November 1, 2026, Law 2 (5922), clip 1132, 11:48:36 PM Mountain: “5J53, I'll be 10-8.” Transcript not yet marked verified.
10-17
En route. November 1, 2026, Law 2 (5922), clip 891, 7:50:32 PM Mountain: “[Please?] show me 10-17 to address…” Transcript excerpt; not yet marked verified.
Disregard / 10-22
Cancel or ignore the previous message or request. November 2, 2026, AFPD car-to-car (6001), clip 45, 1:26:32 PM Mountain: “They're able to push it out of the way, you can 10-22.” Transcript marked verified.
10-23
Arrived at the scene. November 1, 2026, Law 2 (5922), clip 886, 7:47:41 PM Mountain: “[7J29?], 10-23.” Transcript not yet marked verified; the unit identifier is uncertain.
10-28
Vehicle ownership or registration information. Heard in the calls by the site owner; an exact dated clip has not yet been recorded here.
Copy
Message received or understood. An acknowledgment does not necessarily identify the person being addressed.
Affirm / Affirmative
Yes. A short reply may need the preceding clip to make sense.
Negative
No. A short reply may need the preceding clip to make sense.
RP
Usually the reporting party: the person who reported the incident. It describes a role in the call, not necessarily a victim or suspect.
PD
Police department. “At the PD” commonly means at the police station.
Car-to-car
Officer-to-officer radio conversation. The archive on this site covers AFPD’s car-to-car talkgroup, 6001.

State of Utah 10-Code referenceCommon radio abbreviations

Officer names, call signs, and radio IDs

Officer and radio-ID associations from accepted named exchanges and dated Law 2 call-sign evidence. Two or three accepted evidence dates support a possible association; four or more support a strong association. A confirmation for one day applies to that date. Unreviewed candidates do not increase association strength.

Production’s strong associations and dated confirmations are retained across all days, with their original verification attribution. A confirmed date takes priority over the usual radio association on that date. New AI and transcript candidates remain in the downloadable CSVs until reviewed and cannot displace these retained entries.

Two or three accepted evidence dates support a possible association; four or more support a strong association. Strong and possible names are not mixed for the same radio. Two accepted possible associations may coexist. A confirmation for one day does not establish a permanent association or verify every transcript.

Listen, edit transcripts, confirm associations, and export your review. New AI search leads are available there for verification and do not count toward association thresholds.

Law 2 call signs and the directories used to identify an officer are retained with their dates in the evidence below. A call sign applies to its dated assignment; a radio may be used by different officers on different dates.

View official AFPD directories and call sign assignments.

The numeric radio ID beside a clip identifies a transmitting radio. A spoken call sign identifies a unit or agency; a talkgroup identifies the conversation channel.

An officer may have used an ID in these exchanges without being the speaker in every recording under that ID. Attributing a particular statement to an officer still requires further confirmation.

Evidence days count distinct dates with accepted evidence. The CSV also retains unreviewed candidates. Repeated clips do not increase the day count.

Sources and downloads

References used in the guide above. Utah County is listed first for local lookups; the statewide reference covers other agencies.

  • Utah County radio reference

    RadioReference Wiki · Website

    Local reference for agency call signs, law enforcement coverage, and other Utah County radio information.

  • Utah State Agency Radio Call Identifiers

    RadioReference Wiki · Website

    Look up agency call identifiers throughout Utah, including Utah County’s J identifiers.

  • State of Utah 10-Code reference

    Ogden City Police Department · PDF

    The State of Utah 10-Code table on page 8 includes 10-14 (information) and 10-21 (call by phone). This is a Utah reference, not an AFPD-specific codebook.

  • Common radio abbreviations

    California Highway Patrol · Website

    Reference for common abbreviations such as RP and PD. Meanings can vary with context.
